only example values for different clock configurations. Other configurations allowing optimum clock precision are possible.</p></div></div>


<a id="XREF_32126__28_6_4_I2S_master_mode23"></a>

### 42.7.5 I2S master mode

The I2S can be configured in master mode. This means that the serial clock is generated on the CK pin as well as the Word Select signal WS. Master clock (MCK) may be output or not, controlled by the MCKOE bit in the SPIx\_I2SPR register.

#### Procedure

1. Select the I2SDIV[7:0] bits in the SPIx\_I2SPR register to define the serial clock baud rate to reach the proper audio sample frequency. The ODD bit in the SPIx\_I2SPR register also has to be defined.
2. Select the CKPOL bit to define the steady level for the communication clock. Set the MCKOE bit in the SPIx\_I2SPR register if the master clock MCK needs to be provided to the external DAC/ADC audio component (the I2SDIV and ODD values should be computed depending on the state of the MCK output, for more details refer to [Section 42.7.4: Clock generator](#XREF_17705__24_7_4_Clock_generator23)).
3. Set the I2SMOD bit in the SPIx\_I2SCFGR register to activate the I2S functions and choose the I2S standard through the I2SSTD[1:0] and PCMSYNC bits, the data length through the DATLEN[1:0] bits and the number of bits per channel by configuring the CHLEN bit. Select also the I2S master mode and direction (Transmitter or Receiver) through the I2SCFG[1:0] bits in the SPIx\_I2SCFGR register.
4. If needed, select all the potential interrupt sources and the DMA capabilities by writing the SPIx\_CR2 register.
5. The I2SE bit in SPIx\_I2SCFGR register must be set.

WS and CK are configured in output mode. MCK is also an output, if the MCKOE bit in SPIx\_I2SPR is set.

#### Transmission sequence

The transmission sequence begins when a half-word is written into the Tx buffer.

Lets assume the first data written into the Tx buffer corresponds to the left channel data. When data are transferred from the Tx buffer to the shift register, TXE is set and data corresponding to the right channel have to be written into the Tx buffer. The CHSIDE flag indicates which channel is to be transmitted. It has a meaning when the TXE flag is set because the CHSIDE flag is updated when TXE goes high.

A full frame has to be considered as a left channel data transmission followed by a right channel data transmission. It is not possible to have a partial frame where only the left channel is sent.

The data half-word is parallel loaded into the 16-bit shift register during the first bit transmission, and then shifted out, serially, to the MOSI/SD pin, MSB first. The TXE flag is set after each transfer from the Tx buffer to the shift register and an interrupt is generated if the TXEIE bit in the SPIx\_CR2 register is set.

For more details about the write operations depending on the I2S standard mode selected, refer to [Section 42.7.2: Supported audio protocols](#XREF_22707__19_4_2_Supported_audio_protocols23)).

To ensure a continuous audio data transmission, it is mandatory to write the SPIx\_DR register with the next data to transmit before the end of the current transmission.

To switch off the I2S, by clearing I2SE, it is mandatory to wait for TXE = 1 and BSY = 0.

#### Reception sequence

The operating mode is the same as for transmission mode except for the point 3 (refer to the procedure described in [Section 42.7.5: I2S master mode](#XREF_32126__28_6_4_I2S_master_mode23)), where the configuration should set the master reception mode through the I2SCFG[1:0] bits.

Whatever the data or channel length, the audio data are received by 16-bit packets. This means that each time the Rx buffer is full, the RXNE flag is set and an interrupt is generated if the RXNEIE bit is set in SPIx\_CR2 register. Depending on the data and channel length configuration, the audio value received for a right or left channel may result from one or two receptions into the Rx buffer.

Clearing the RXNE bit is performed by reading the SPIx\_DR register.

CHSIDE is updated after each reception. It is sensitive to the WS signal generated by the I2S cell.

For more details about the read operations depending on the I2S standard mode selected, refer to [Section 42.7.2: Supported audio protocols](#XREF_22707__19_4_2_Supported_audio_protocols23).

If data are received while the previously received data have not been read yet, an overrun is generated and the OVR flag is set. If the ERRIE bit is set in the SPIx\_CR2 register, an interrupt is generated to indicate the error.

To switch off the I2S, specific actions are required to ensure that the I2S completes the transfer cycle properly without initiating a new data transfer. The sequence depends on the configuration of the data and channel lengths, and on the audio protocol mode selected. In the case of:

- 16-bit data length extended on 32-bit channel length (DATLEN = 00 and CHLEN = 1) using the LSB justified mode (I2SSTD = 10)
   a) Wait for the second to last RXNE = 1 (n – 1)
   b) Then wait 17 I2S clock cycles (using a software loop)
   c) Disable the I2S (I2SE = 0)
- 16-bit data length extended on 32-bit channel length (DATLEN = 00 and CHLEN = 1) in MSB justified, I2S or PCM modes (I2SSTD = 00, I2SSTD = 01 or I2SSTD = 11, respectively)
   a) Wait for the last RXNE
   b) Then wait 1 I2S clock cycle (using a software loop)
   c) Disable the I2S (I2SE = 0)
- For all other combinations of DATLEN and CHLEN, whatever the audio mode selected through the I2SSTD bits, carry out the following sequence to switch off the I2S:
   a) Wait for the second to last RXNE = 1 (n – 1)
   b) Then wait one I2S clock cycle (using a software loop)
   c) Disable the I2S (I2SE = 0)

**Note:**

The BSY flag is kept low during transfers.

<a id="XREF_10417__28_6_5_I2S_slave_mode23"></a>

### 42.7.6 I2S slave mode

For the slave configuration, the I2S can be configured in transmission or reception mode. The operating mode is following mainly the same rules as described for the I2S master configuration. In slave mode, there is no clock to be generated by the I2S interface. The clock and WS signals are input from the external master connected to the I2S interface. There is then no need, for the user, to configure the clock.

The configuration steps to follow are listed below:

1. Set the I2SMOD bit in the SPIx\_I2SCFGR register to select I2S mode and choose the I2S standard through the I2SSTD[1:0] bits, the data length through the DATLEN[1:0] bits and the number of bits per channel for the frame configuring the CHLEN bit. Select also the mode (transmission or reception) for the slave through the I2SCFG[1:0] bits in SPIx\_I2SCFGR register.
2. If needed, select all the potential interrupt sources and the DMA capabilities by writing the SPIx\_CR2 register.
3. The I2SE bit in SPIx\_I2SCFGR register must be set.

#### Transmission sequence

The transmission sequence begins when the external master device sends the clock and when the NSS\_WS signal requests the transfer of data. The slave has to be enabled before the external master starts the communication. The I2S data register has to be loaded before the master initiates the communication.

For the I2S, MSB justified and LSB justified modes, the first data item to be written into the data register corresponds to the data for the left channel. When the communication starts, the data are transferred from the Tx buffer to the shift register. The TXE flag is then set in order to request the right channel data to be written into the I2S data register.

The CHSIDE flag indicates which channel is to be transmitted. Compared to the master transmission mode, in slave mode, CHSIDE is sensitive to the WS signal coming from the external master. This means that the slave needs to be ready to transmit the first data before the clock is generated by the master. WS assertion corresponds to left channel transmitted first.

**Note:**

The I2SE has to be written at least two PCLK cycles before the first clock of the master comes on the CK line.

The data half-word is parallel-loaded into the 16-bit shift register (from the internal bus) during the first bit transmission, and then shifted out serially to the MOSI/SD pin MSB first. The TXE flag is set after each transfer from the Tx buffer to the shift register and an interrupt is generated if the TXEIE bit in the SPIx\_CR2 register is set.

Note that the TXE flag should be checked to be at 1 before attempting to write the Tx buffer.

For more details about the write operations depending on the I2S standard mode selected, refer to [Section 42.7.2: Supported audio protocols](#XREF_22707__19_4_2_Supported_audio_protocols23).

To secure a continuous audio data transmission, it is mandatory to write the SPIx\_DR register with the next data to transmit before the end of the current transmission. An underrun flag is set and an interrupt may be generated if the data are not written into the SPIx\_DR register before the first clock edge of the next data communication. This indicates to the software that the transferred data are wrong. If the ERRIE bit is set into the SPIx\_CR2 register, an interrupt is generated when the UDR flag in the SPIx\_SR register goes high. In this case, it is mandatory to switch off the I2S and to restart a data transfer starting from the left channel.

To switch off the I2S, by clearing the I2SE bit, it is mandatory to wait for TXE = 1 and BSY = 0.

#### Reception sequence

The operating mode is the same as for the transmission mode except for the point 1 (refer to the procedure described in [Section 42.7.6: I2S slave mode](#XREF_10417__28_6_5_I2S_slave_mode23)), where the configuration should set the master reception mode using the I2SCFG[1:0] bits in the SPIx\_I2SCFGR register.

Whatever the data length or the channel length, the audio data are received by 16-bit packets. This means that each time the RX buffer is full, the RXNE flag in the SPIx\_SR register is set and an interrupt is generated if the RXNEIE bit is set in the SPIx\_CR2 register. Depending on the data length and channel length configuration, the audio value received for a right or left channel may result from one or two receptions into the RX buffer.

The CHSIDE flag is updated each time data are received to be read from the SPIx\_DR register. It is sensitive to the external WS line managed by the external master component.

Clearing the RXNE bit is performed by reading the SPIx\_DR register.

For more details about the read operations depending the I2S standard mode selected, refer to [Section 42.7.2: Supported audio protocols](#XREF_22707__19_4_2_Supported_audio_protocols23).

If data are received while the preceding received data have not yet been read, an overrun is generated and the OVR flag is set. If the bit ERRIE is set in the SPIx\_CR2 register, an interrupt is generated to indicate the error.

To switch off the I2S in reception mode, I2SE has to be cleared immediately after receiving the last RXNE = 1.

**Note:**

The external master components should have the capability of sending/receiving data in 16-bit or 32-bit packets via an audio channel.

### 42.7.7 I2S status flags

Three status flags are provided for the application to fully monitor the state of the I2S bus.

#### Busy flag (BSY)

The BSY flag is set and cleared by hardware (writing to this flag has no effect). It indicates the state of the communication layer of the I2S.

When BSY is set, it indicates that the I2S is busy communicating. There is one exception in master receive mode (I2SCFG = 11) where the BSY flag is kept low during reception.

The BSY flag is useful to detect the end of a transfer if the software needs to disable the I2S. This avoids corrupting the last transfer. For this, the procedure described below must be strictly respected.

The BSY flag is set when a transfer starts, except when the I2S is in master receiver mode.

The BSY flag is cleared:

- When a transfer completes (except in master transmit mode, in which the communication is supposed to be continuous)
- When the I2S is disabled

When communication is continuous:

- In master transmit mode, the BSY flag is kept high during all the transfers
- In slave mode, the BSY flag goes low for one I2S clock cycle between each transfer

**Note:**

Do not use the BSY flag to handle each data transmission or reception. It is better to use the TXE and RXNE flags instead.

#### Tx buffer empty flag (TXE)

When set, this flag indicates that the Tx buffer is empty and the next data to be transmitted can then be loaded into it. The TXE flag is reset when the Tx buffer already contains data to be transmitted. It is also reset when the I2S is disabled (I2SE bit is reset).

#### RX buffer not empty (RXNE)

When set, this flag indicates that there are valid received data in the RX Buffer. It is reset when SPIx\_DR register is read.

#### Channel Side flag (CHSIDE)

In transmission mode, this flag is refreshed when TXE goes high. It indicates the channel side to which the data to transfer on SD has to belong. In case of an underrun error event in slave transmission mode, this flag is not reliable and I2S needs to be switched off and switched on before resuming the communication.

In reception mode, this flag is refreshed when data are received into SPIx\_DR. It indicates from which channel side data have been received. Note that in case of error (like OVR) this flag becomes meaningless and the I2S should be reset by disabling and then enabling it (with configuration if it needs changing).

This flag has no meaning in the PCM standard (for both Short and Long frame modes).

When the OVR or UDR flag in the SPIx\_SR is set and the ERRIE bit in SPIx\_CR2 is also set, an interrupt is generated. This interrupt can be cleared by reading the SPIx\_SR status register (once the interrupt source has been cleared).

### 42.7.8 I2S error flags

There are three error flags for the I2S cell.

#### Underrun flag (UDR)

In slave transmission mode this flag is set when the first clock for data transmission appears while the software has not yet loaded any value into SPIx\_DR. It is available when the I2SMOD bit in the SPIx\_I2SCFGR register is set. An interrupt may be generated if the ERRIE bit in the SPIx\_CR2 register is set.  
The UDR bit is cleared by a read operation on the SPIx\_SR register.

#### Overrun flag (OVR)

This flag is set when data are received and the previous data have not yet been read from the SPIx\_DR register. As a result, the incoming data are lost. An interrupt may be generated if the ERRIE bit is set in the SPIx\_CR2 register.

In this case, the receive buffer contents are not updated with the newly received data from the transmitter device. A read operation to the SPIx\_DR register returns the previous correctly received data. All other subsequently transmitted half-words are lost.

Clearing the OVR bit is done by a read operation on the SPIx\_DR register followed by a read access to the SPIx\_SR register.

#### Frame error flag (FRE)

This flag can be set by hardware only if the I2S is configured in Slave mode. It is set if the external master is changing the WS line while the slave is not expecting this change. If the synchronization is lost, the following steps are required to recover from this state and resynchronize the external master device with the I2S slave device:

1. Disable the I2S.
2. Enable it again when the correct level is detected on the WS line (WS line is high in I2S mode or low for MSB- or LSB-justified or PCM modes.

Desynchronization between master and slave devices may be due to noisy environment on the CK communication clock or on the WS frame synchronization line. An error interrupt can be generated if the ERRIE bit is set. The desynchronization flag (FRE) is cleared by software when the status register is read.

### 42.7.9 DMA features

In I2S mode, the DMA works in exactly the same way as it does in SPI mode. There is no difference except that the CRC feature is not available in I2S mode since there is no data transfer protection system.

## 42.8 I2S interrupts

[Table 393](#XREF_67928__Table_78__I2S_interrupt_requests23) provides the list of I2S interrupts.

## 42.9 SPI and I2S registers

The peripheral registers can be accessed by half-words (16-bit) or words (32-bit). SPI\_DR in addition can be accessed by 8-bit access.

### 42.9.1 SPI control register 1 (SPIx\_CR1)

Address offset: 0x00

Reset value: 0x0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.50px"/><col width="499.67px"/><tr height="121.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">BIDIMODE:</span><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> </span>Bidirectional data mode enable. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables half-duplex communication using common single bidirectional data line. Keep RXONLY bit clear when bidirectional mode is active.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="font-style:Italic;"><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">0: 2-line unidirectional data mode selected</span></p><p class="TableIndent">1: 1-line bidirectional data mode selected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="121.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BIDIOE:</span> Output enable in bidirectional mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit combined with the BIDIMODE bit selects the direction of transfer in bidirectional mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Output disabled (receive-only mode) </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Output enabled (transmit-only mode)</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>In master mode, the MOSI pin is used and in slave mode, the MISO pin is used.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="90.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CRCEN:</span> Hardware CRC calculation enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: CRC calculation disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: CRC calculation enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit should be written only when SPI is disabled (SPE = ‘0’) for correct operation.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="90.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CRCNEXT:</span> Transmit CRC next</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Next transmit value is from Tx buffer.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Next transmit value is from Tx CRC register.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit has to be written as soon as the last data is written in the SPIx_DR register.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="106.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CRCL:</span> CRC length</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software to select the CRC length.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: 8-bit CRC length</p><p class="TableIndent">1: 16-bit CRC length</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit should be written only when SPI is disabled (SPE = ‘0’) for correct operation.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXONLY:</span> Receive only mode enabled. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables simplex communication using a single unidirectional line to receive data exclusively. Keep BIDIMODE bit clear when receive only mode is active.This bit is also useful in a multislave system in which this particular slave is not accessed, the output from the accessed slave is not corrupted.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Full-duplex (Transmit and receive)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Output disabled (Receive-only mode)</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="89.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SSM: </span>Software slave management</p><p class="TableIndent">When the SSM bit is set, the NSS pin input is replaced with the value from the SSI bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Software slave management disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Software slave management enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is not used in <span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode</span><span class="Bold" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">and </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">SPI TI mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SSI:</span> Internal slave select</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit has an effect only when the SSM bit is set. The value of this bit is forced onto the NSS pin and the I/O value of the NSS pin is ignored.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is not used in <span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode</span><span class="Bold" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">and </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">SPI TI mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="90.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">LSBFIRST</span><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> </span>Frame format</p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">0: data is transmitted / received with the MSB first</span></p><p class="TableIndent">1: <span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">data is transmitted / received with the LSB first</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>1. This bit should not be changed when communication is ongoing.</p><p class="RegTNote_">2. This bit is not used in <span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode</span><span class="Bold" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">and </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">SPI TI mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="105.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SPE: </span>SPI enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Peripheral disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Peripheral enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When disabling the SPI, follow the procedure described in <a href="#XREF_75008__Procedure_for_disabling_the_SPI23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Procedure for disabling the SPI on page 1840</a>. </p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="186.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 5:3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BR[2:0]:</span> Baud rate control</p><p class="TableIndent">000: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/2</p><p class="TableIndent">001: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/4</p><p class="TableIndent">010: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/8</p><p class="TableIndent">011: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/16</p><p class="TableIndent">100: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/32</p><p class="TableIndent">101: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/64</p><p class="TableIndent">110: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/128</p><p class="TableIndent">111: f<sub>PCLK</sub>/256</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits should not be changed when communication is ongoing.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">These bits are not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" height="90.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MSTR:</span> Master selection<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Slave configuration</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Master <div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.50px"/><col width="498.67px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="BOLD" style="font-weight:Bold;">LDMA_TX:</span> Last DMA transfer for transmission</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used in data packing mode, to define if the total number of data to transmit by DMA is odd or even. It has significance only if the TXDMAEN bit in the SPIx_CR2 register is set and if packing mode is used (data length =&lt; 8-bit and write access to SPIx_DR is 16-bit wide). It has to be written when the SPI is disabled (SPE = 0 in the SPIx_CR1 register).</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Number of data to transfer is even</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Number of data to transfer is odd</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Refer to <a href="#XREF_75008__Procedure_for_disabling_the_SPI23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Procedure for disabling the SPI on page 1840</a> if the CRCEN bit is set.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">This bit is not used in I²S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">LDMA_RX</span>: Last DMA transfer for reception</p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="black" style="font-size : 10.00pt;">Th</span>is bit is used in data packing mode, to define if the total number of data to receive by DMA is odd or even. It has significance only if the RXDMAEN bit in the SPIx_CR2 register is set and if packing mode is used (data length =&lt; 8-bit and write access to SPIx_DR is 16-bit wide). It has to be written when the SPI is disabled (SPE = 0 in the SPIx_CR1 register).</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Number of data to transfer is even</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Number of data to transfer is odd</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Refer to <a href="#XREF_75008__Procedure_for_disabling_the_SPI23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Procedure for disabling the SPI on page 1840</a> if the CRCEN bit is set.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">This bit is not used in I²S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="106.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="BOLD" style="font-weight:Bold;">FRXTH</span>: FIFO reception threshold</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used to set the threshold of the RXFIFO that triggers an RXNE event </p><p class="TableIndent">0: RXNE event is generated if the FIFO level is greater than or equal to 1/2 (16-bit)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: RXNE event is generated if the FIFO level is greater than or equal to 1/4 (8-bit)</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="361.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DS[3:0]</span>: Data size</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits configure the data length for SPI transfers.</p><p class="TableIndent">0000: Not used</p><p class="TableIndent">0001: Not used</p><p class="TableIndent">0010: Not used</p><p class="TableIndent">0011: 4-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0100: 5-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0101: 6-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0110: 7-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0111: 8-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1000: 9-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1001: 10-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1010: 11-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1011: 12-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1100: 13-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1101: 14-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1110: 15-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">1111: 16-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">If software attempts to write one of the “Not used” values, they are forced to the value “0111”<br/>(8-bit)</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">These bits are not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXEIE:</span> Tx buffer empty interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: TXE interrupt masked </p><p class="TableIndent">1: TXE interrupt not masked. Used to generate an interrupt request when the TXE flag is set.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXNEIE:</span> RX buffer not empty interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: RXNE interrupt masked </p><p class="TableIndent">1: RXNE interrupt not masked. Used to generate an interrupt request when the RXNE flag is set.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,goldfish,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ERRIE:</span> Error interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit controls the generation of an interrupt when an error condition occurs (CRCERR, OVR, MODF in SPI mode, FRE at TI mode<span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"> and UDR, OVR, and FRE in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="Bigbirdie,Birdie,Goldfish,LittleDory,Littlebirdie,Mantaedge,RM0453_Bee,RM0461_Bee,RM0490_Spider,RM0440,STM32G0x0_OrcaZero128K,STM32G0x1_OrcaZero128K,Stingray" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode</span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">).</span></p><p class="TableIndent" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">0: Error interrupt is masked</p><p class="TableIndent" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">1: Error interrupt is enabled</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="90.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FRF</span>: Frame format</p><p class="TableIndent">0: SPI Motorola mode</p><p class="TableIndent">1 SPI TI mode</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit must be written only when the SPI is disabled (SPE=0).</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="154.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NSSP</span><span style="font-size : 7.00pt;">: </span>NSS pulse management</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used in master mode only. it allows the SPI to generate an NSS pulse between two consecutive data when doing continuous transfers. In the case of a single data transfer, it forces the NSS pin high level after the transfer. </p><p class="TableBodyL">It has no meaning if CPHA = ’1’, or FRF = ’1’.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No NSS pulse</p><p class="TableIndent">1: NSS pulse generated</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>1. This bit must be written only when the SPI is disabled (SPE=0).</p><p class="RegTNote_">2. This bit is not used in <span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="Bigbirdie,Birdie,Goldfish,LittleDory,Littlebirdie,Mantaedge,RM0453_Bee,RM0461_Bee,RM0490_Spider,RM0440,STM32G0x0_OrcaZero128K,STM32G0x1_OrcaZero128K,Stingray" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode and </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">SPI TI mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SSOE:</span> SS output enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: SS output is disabled in master mode and the SPI interface can work in multimaster configuration</p><p class="TableIndent">1: SS output is enabled in master mode and when the SPI interface is enabled. The SPI interface cannot work in a multimaster environment.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is not used in <span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode</span><span class="Bold" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">and </span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">SPI TI mode.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXDMAEN:</span> Tx buffer DMA enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, a DMA request is generated whenever the TXE flag is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Tx buffer DMA disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Tx buffer DMA enabled</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXDMAEN:</span> Rx buffer DMA enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, a DMA request is generated whenever the RXNE flag is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Rx buffer DMA disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Rx buffer DMA enabled</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 42.9.3 SPI status register (SPIx\_SR)

Address offset: 0x08

Reset value: 0x0002

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.17px"/><col width="499.01px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="138.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 12:11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FTLVL[1:0]:</span> FIFO transmission level</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by hardware. </p><p class="TableIndent">00: FIFO empty</p><p class="TableIndent">01: 1/4 FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">10: 1/2 FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">11: FIFO full (considered as FULL when the FIFO threshold is greater than 1/2)</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">This bit is not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> </span><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; <div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="116.10px"/><col width="498.07px"/><tr height="132.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DR[15:0]:</span> Data register</p><p class="TableIndent">Data received or to be transmitted</p><p class="TableIndent">The data register serves as an interface between the Rx and Tx FIFOs. When the data register is read, RxFIFO is accessed while the write to data register accesses TxFIFO (See <a href="#XREF_53041__1_3_2_SPI_Slave_Mode23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 42.5.9: Data transmission and reception procedures</a>).</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Data is always right-aligned. Unused bits are ignored when writing to the register, and read as zero when the register is read. The Rx threshold setting must always correspond with the read access currently used.</p></td></tr></table></div>

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.57px"/><col width="498.60px"/><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CRCPOLY[15:0]:</span> CRC polynomial register</p><p class="TableIndent">This register contains the polynomial for the CRC calculation.</p><p class="TableIndent">The CRC polynomial (0x0007) is the reset value of this register. Another polynomial can be configured as required.</p></td></tr></table></div>


**Note:**

The polynomial value should be odd only. No even value is supported.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.48px"/><col width="499.69px"/><tr height="209.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXCRC[15:0]:</span> Rx CRC register</p><p class="TableIndent">When CRC calculation is enabled, the RXCRC[15:0] bits contain the computed CRC value of the subsequently received bytes. This register is reset when the CRCEN bit in SPIx_CR1 register is written to 1. The CRC is calculated serially using the polynomial programmed in the SPIx_CRCPR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">Only the 8 LSB bits are considered when the CRC frame format is set to be 8-bit length (CRCL bit in the SPIx_CR1 is cleared). CRC calculation is done based on any CRC8 standard.</p><p class="TableIndent">The entire 16-bits of this register are considered when a 16-bit CRC frame format is selected (CRCL bit in the SPIx_CR1 register is set). CRC calculation is done based on any CRC16 standard.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>A read to this register when the BSY Flag is set could return an incorrect value.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">These bits are not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode.</span></p></td></tr></table></div>

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.51px"/><col width="498.66px"/><tr height="209.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXCRC[15:0]:</span> Tx CRC register</p><p class="TableIndent">When CRC calculation is enabled, the TXCRC[7:0] bits contain the computed CRC value of the subsequently transmitted bytes. This register is reset when the CRCEN bit of SPIx_CR1 is written to 1. The CRC is calculated serially using the polynomial programmed in the SPIx_CRCPR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Only the 8 LSB bits are considered when the CRC frame format is set to be 8-bit length (CRCL bit in the SPIx_CR1 is cleared). CRC calculation is done based on any CRC8 standard.</p><p class="TableIndent">The entire 16-bits of this register are considered when a 16-bit CRC frame format is selected (CRCL bit in the SPIx_CR1 register is set). CRC calculation is done based on any CRC16 standard.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>A read to this register when the BSY flag is set could return an incorrect value.</p><p class="RegTNote_"><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">These bits are not used in I</span><sup data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><span class="Superscript" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup><span data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">S mode.</span></p></td></tr></table></div>

data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="80.27px"/><col width="533.91px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:<span data-conditional="_spi2s1_astrten,_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k">13</span></p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_spi2s1_astrten,_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k" height="198.67px"><td data-conditional="_spi2s1_astrten,_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td data-conditional="_spi2s1_astrten,_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ASTRTEN</span>: Asynchronous start enable.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: The Asynchronous start is disabled. </p><p class="TableIndent">When the I2S is enabled in slave mode, the hardware starts the transfer when the I2S clock is received and an appropriate transition is detected on the WS signal.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: The Asynchronous start is enabled. </p><p class="TableIndent">When the I2S is enabled in slave mode, the hardware starts the transfer when the I2S clock is received and the appropriate level is detected on the WS signal.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>The appropriate <span style="font-weight:Bold;">transition</span> is a falling edge on WS signal when I<sup><span class="Superscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup>S Philips Standard is used, or a rising edge for other standards.</p><p class="RegTNote_">The appropriate <span style="font-weight:Bold;">level </span>is a low level on WS signal when I<sup><span class="Superscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup>S Philips Standard is used, or a high level for other standards.</p><p class="RegTNote_">Please refer to <a href="#XREF_13274__19_4_3_Clock_generator23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 42.7.3: Start-up description</a> for additional information.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2SMOD</span>: I2S mode selection</p><p class="TableIndent">0: SPI mode is selected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: I2S mode is selected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit should be configured when the SPI is disabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2SE</span>: I2S enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: I2S peripheral is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: I2S peripheral is enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="122.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 9:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2SCFG[1:0]</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>I2S configuration mode</p><p class="TableIndent">00: Slave - transmit</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Slave - receive</p><p class="TableIndent">10: Master - transmit</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Master - receive</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits should be configured when the I2S is disabled.</p><p class="RegTNote_">They are not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="90.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PCMSYNC</span>: PCM frame synchronization</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Short frame synchronization</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Long frame synchronization</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit has a meaning only if I2SSTD = 11 (PCM standard is used).</p><p class="RegTNote_">It is not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="140.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 5:4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2SSTD[1:0]</span>: I2S standard selection</p><p class="TableIndent">00: I<sup><span class="Superscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup>S Philips standard</p><p class="TableIndent">01: MSB justified standard (left justified)</p><p class="TableIndent">10: LSB justified standard (right justified)</p><p class="TableIndent">11: PCM standard</p><p class="TableBodyL">For more details on I<sup><span class="Superscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">2</span></sup>S standards, refer to <a href="#XREF_22707__19_4_2_Supported_audio_protocols23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 42.7.2 on page 1856</a></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>For correct operation, these bits should be configured when the I2S is disabled.</p><p class="RegTNote_">They are not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="122.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CKPOL</span>: Inactive state clock polarity</p><p class="TableIndent">0: I2S clock inactive state is low level</p><p class="TableIndent">1: I2S clock inactive state is high level</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>For correct operation, this bit should be configured when the I2S is disabled.</p><p class="RegTNote_">It is not used in SPI mode.</p><p class="RegTNote_">The bit CKPOL does not affect the CK edge sensitivity used to receive or transmit the SD and WS signals.</p></td></tr><tr height="122.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DATLEN[1:0]</span>: Data length to be transferred</p><p class="TableIndent">00: 16-bit data length</p><p class="TableIndent">01: 24-bit data length</p><p class="TableIndent">10: 32-bit data length</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Not allowed</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>For correct operation, these bits should be configured when the I2S is disabled.</p><p class="RegTNote_">They are not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="121.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CHLEN</span>: Channel length (number of bits per audio channel)</p><p class="TableIndent">0: 16-bit wide</p><p class="TableIndent">1: 32-bit wide</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit write operation has a meaning only if DATLEN = 00 otherwise the channel length is fixed to 32-bit by hardware whatever the value filled in.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>For correct operation, this bit should be configured when the I2S is disabled.</p><p class="RegTNote_">It is not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr></table></div>

Reset value: 0x0002

<div align="left" data-conditional="_spi2s1_i2s,bigbirdie,birdie,goldfish,littlebirdie,littledory,mantaedge,rm0453_bee,rm0461_bee,rm0490_spider,RM0440,stingray,stm32g0x0_orcazero128k,stm32g0x1_orcazero128k"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="78.52px"/><col width="535.65px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="105.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MCKOE</span>: Master clock output enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Master clock output is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Master clock output is enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit should be configured when the I2S is disabled. It is used only when the I2S is in master mode.</p><p class="RegTNote_">It is not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="121.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ODD</span>: Odd factor for the prescaler</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Real divider value is = I2SDIV *2</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Real divider value is = (I2SDIV * 2) + 1</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_13274__19_4_3_Clock_generator23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 42.7.3 on page 1863</a>.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit should be configured when the I2S is disabled. It is used only when the I2S is in master mode.</p><p class="RegTNote_">It is not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="105.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">I2SDIV[7:0]</span>: I2S linear prescaler</p><p class="TableIndent">I2SDIV [7:0] = 0 or I2SDIV [7:0] = 1 are forbidden values.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_13274__19_4_3_Clock_generator23" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 42.7.3 on page 1863</a>.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>These bits should be configured when the I2S is disabled. They are used only when the I2S is in master mode.</p><p class="RegTNote_">They are not used in SPI mode.</p></td></tr></table></div>
